In a *Washington Post* column, [Erik Wemple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erik_Wemple) said Chris Wallace had covered *The New York Times* story himself on *Fox News Sunday*, adding: "Here's another case of the differing standards between Fox News's opinion operation", which has given "a state-run vibe on all matters related to Trump", compared to Fox News's news operation, which has provided "mostly sane coverage".[^cite_note-298]

### Climate change

Further information: [Climate change denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_denial) and [Media coverage of climate change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_coverage_of_climate_change)

Fox News has often been described as a major platform for [climate change denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_denial).[^cite_note-Climate_Denial-46] A 2011 study by Lauren Feldman and [Anthony Leiserowitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Leiserowitz) found Fox News "takes a more dismissive tone toward climate change than CNN and MSNBC".[^cite_note-Feldman-2012-303] A 2008 study found Fox News emphasized the scientific uncertainty of climate change more than CNN, was less likely to say climate change was real, and more likely to interview climate change skeptics.[^cite_note-Feldman-2012-303] Leaked emails showed that in 2009 [Bill Sammon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Sammon), the Fox News Washington managing editor, instructed Fox News journalists to dispute the [scientific consensus on climate change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_consensus_on_climate_change) and "refrain from asserting that the planet has warmed (or cooled) in any given period without IMMEDIATELY pointing out that such theories are based upon data that critics have called into question."[^cite_note-304]

According to climate scientist [Michael E. Mann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_E._Mann), Fox News "has constructed an alternative universe where the laws of physics no longer apply, where the [greenhouse effect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenhouse_effect) is a myth, and where climate change is a hoax, the product of a massive conspiracy among scientists, who somehow have gotten the polar bears, glaciers, sea levels, superstorms, and megadroughts to play along."[^cite_note-Mann-2016-301] According to [James Lawrence Powell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Lawrence_Powell)'s 2011 study of the [climate science denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_science_denial) movement, Fox News provides "the deniers with a platform to say whatever they like without fear of contradiction."[^cite_note-Powell-2011-302] Fox News employs [Steve Milloy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Milloy), a prominent [climate change denier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_denier) with close financial and organizational ties to oil companies, as a contributor. In his columns about climate change for *FoxNews.com*, Fox News has failed to disclose his substantial funding from oil companies.[^cite_note-handful-305]

In 2011, the hosts of *Fox & Friends* described climate change as "unproven science", a "disputed fact", and criticized the [Department of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Education) for working together with the children's network [Nickelodeon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nickelodeon) to teach children about climate change.[^cite_note-306] In 2001, Sean Hannity described the [scientific consensus on climate change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_consensus_on_climate_change) as "phony science from the left".[^cite_note-McKnight-2010-307] In 2004, he falsely alleged that "scientists still can't agree on whether the global warming is scientific fact or fiction".[^cite_note-McKnight-2010-307] In 2010, Hannity said the so-called ["Climategate"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climatic_Research_Unit_email_controversy) – the leaking of e-mails by climate scientist that climate change skeptics claimed demonstrated scientific misconduct but which all subsequent enquiries have found no evidence of misconduct or wrongdoing – a "scandal" that "exposed global warming as a myth cooked up by alarmists".[^cite_note-308] Hannity frequently invites contrarian fringe scientists and critics of climate change to his shows.[^cite_note-309] In 2019, a widely shared Fox News news report falsely claimed that new climate science research showed that the Earth might be heading to a new Ice Age; the author of the study that Fox News cited said that Fox News "utterly misrepresents our research" and the study did not in any way suggest that Earth was heading to an Ice Age. Fox News later corrected the story.[^cite_note-310]

[Shepard Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shepard_Smith) drew attention for being one of few voices formerly on Fox News to forcefully state that climate change is real, that human activities are a primary contributor to it and that there is a [scientific consensus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_consensus_on_climate_change) on the issue.[^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312] His acceptance of the scientific consensus on climate change drew criticism from Fox News viewers and conservatives.[^cite_note-313][^cite_note-314] Smith left Fox News in October 2019.[^cite_note-315] In a 2021 interview with [Christiane Amanpour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christiane_Amanpour) on her [eponymous show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amanpour_&_Company) on CNN, he stated that his presence on Fox had become "untenable" due to the "falsehoods" and "lies" intentionally spread on the network's opinion shows.[^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317]

### Murder of Seth Rich conspiracy

See also: [Murder of Seth Rich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murder_of_Seth_Rich)

On May 16, 2017, a day when other news organizations were extensively covering [Donald Trump's revelation of classified information to Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_revelation_of_classified_information_to_Russia),[^cite_note-Snopes-2017-318] Fox News ran a lead story about a private investigator's uncorroborated claims about the [murder of Seth Rich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murder_of_Seth_Rich), a [DNC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_National_Committee) staffer.[^cite_note-Seitz-Wald-2017-319][^cite_note-Hermann-2017-320][^cite_note-Darcy-2017-321] The private investigator said he had uncovered evidence that Rich was in contact with WikiLeaks and law enforcement were covering it up.[^cite_note-Seitz-Wald-2017-319] The killing of Rich has given rise to [conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conspiracy_theories) in right-wing circles that Hillary Clinton and the Democratic Party had Seth Rich killed allegedly because he was the source of the [DNC leaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DNC_leaks).[^cite_note-Seitz-Wald-2017-319] U.S. intelligence agencies determined Russia was the source of the leaks.[^cite_note-322] In reporting the investigator's claims, the Fox News report reignited right-wing conspiracy theories about the killing.[^cite_note-Seitz-Wald-2017-319][^cite_note-Darcy-2017-321]

The Fox News story fell apart within hours.[^cite_note-Darcy-2018-323] Other news organizations quickly revealed the investigator was a Donald Trump supporter and had according to NBC News "developed a reputation for making outlandish claims, such as one appearance on Fox News in 2007 in which he warned that underground networks of pink pistol-toting lesbian gangs were raping young women."[^cite_note-Seitz-Wald-2017-319][^cite_note-Bromwich-2017-324] The family of Seth Rich, the Washington D.C. police department, the Washington D.C. mayor's office, the FBI, and law enforcement sources familiar with the case rebuked the investigator's claims.[^cite_note-Seitz-Wald-2017-319][^cite_note-Hermann-2017-320] Rich's relatives said: "We are a family who is committed to facts, not fake evidence that surfaces every few months to fill the void and distract law enforcement and the general public from finding Seth's murderers."[^cite_note-Seitz-Wald-2017-319] The spokesperson for the family criticized Fox News for its reporting, alleging the outlet was motivated by a desire to deflect attention from the Trump-Russia story: "I think there's a very special place in hell for people that would use the memory of a murder victim in order to pursue a political agenda."[^cite_note-Snopes-2017-318] The family has called for retractions and apologies from Fox News for the inaccurate reporting.[^cite_note-Bromwich-2017-324][^cite_note-325] Over the course of the day, Fox News altered the contents of the story and the headline, but did not issue corrections.[^cite_note-Bromwich-2017-324][^cite_note-326] When CNN contacted the private investigator later that day, the investigator said he had no evidence that Rich had contacted WikiLeaks.[^cite_note-Darcy-2017-321] The investigator claimed he only learned about the possible existence of the evidence from a Fox News reporter.[^cite_note-Darcy-2017-321] Fox News did not respond to inquiries by CNN, and the Washington Post.[^cite_note-Hermann-2017-320][^cite_note-Darcy-2017-321] Fox News later on May 23, seven days after the story was published, retracted its original report, saying the original report did not meet its standards.[^cite_note-Darcy-2018-323][^cite_note-327]

[Nicole Hemmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicole_Hemmer), then assistant professor at the [Miller Center of Public Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miller_Center_of_Public_Affairs), wrote that the promotion of the conspiracy theory demonstrated how Fox News was "remaking itself in the image of fringe media in the age of Trump, blurring the lines between real and fake news."[^cite_note-328] [Max Boot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Boot) of the [Council on Foreign Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_on_Foreign_Relations) said while intent behind Fox News, as a counterweight to the liberal media was laudable, the culmination of those efforts have been to create an alternative news source that promotes hoaxes and myths, of which the promotion of the Seth Rich conspiracy is an example.[^cite_note-329] Fox News was also criticized by conservative outlets, such as *[The Weekly Standard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Weekly_Standard)*,[^cite_note-330] *[National Review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Review)*,[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332] and conservative columnists, such as [Jennifer Rubin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Rubin_\(columnist\)),[^cite_note-333] [Michael Gerson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Gerson),[^cite_note-334] and [John Podhoretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Podhoretz).[^cite_note-335]

Rich's parents, Joel and Mary Rich, sued Fox News for the emotional distress it had caused them by its false reporting. In 2020, Fox News settled with Rich family, making a payment that was not officially disclosed but which was reported to be in the seven figures.[^cite_note-settlement-336] Although the settlement had been agreed to earlier in the year, Fox News arranged to delay the public announcement until after the 2020 presidential election.[^cite_note-settlement-336][^cite_note-337]

### Unite the Right rally in Charlottesville

Further information: [Unite the Right rally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unite_the_Right_rally)

Fox News hosts and contributors defended Trump's remarks that "many sides" were to blame for violence at a gathering of hundreds of [white nationalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_nationalists) in Charlottesville, Virginia.[^cite_note-Grynbaum-2017a-338][^cite_note-Weigel-2017-339] Some criticized Trump.[^cite_note-Weigel-2017-339][^cite_note-Byers-2017-340] In a press conference on August 15, Trump used the term "alt-left" to describe counterprotesters at the [white supremacist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_supremacist) rally, a term which had been used in Fox News' coverage of the white supremacist rally.[^cite_note-Grynbaum-2017a-338] Several of Trump's comments at the press conference mirrored those appearing earlier on Fox News.[^cite_note-341]

According to [Dylan Byers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dylan_Byers) of CNN, Fox News' coverage on the day of the press conference "was heavy with "[whataboutism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whataboutism)". The average Fox viewer was likely left with the impression that the media's criticism of Trump and leftist protestors' toppling of some Confederate statues were far greater threats to America than white supremacism or the president's apparent defense of bigotry."[^cite_note-Byers-2017-340] Byers wrote "it showed that if Fox News has a line when it comes to Trump's presidency, it was not crossed on Tuesday."[^cite_note-Byers-2017-340]

### Glenn Beck's comments about George Soros

During [Glenn Beck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glenn_Beck)'s tenure at Fox News, he became one of the most high-profile proponents of [conspiracy theories about George Soros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Soros#Conspiracy_theories), a Jewish Hungarian-American businessman and philanthropist known for his donations to [American liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_liberalism_in_the_United_States) political causes.[^cite_note-Lee-2018-342] Beck regularly described Soros as a "puppet-master" and used common [anti-Semitic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Semitic) tropes to describe Soros and his activities.[^cite_note-Lee-2018-342] In a 2010 three-part series, Beck depicted George Soros as a cartoonish villain trying to "form a shadow government, using humanitarian aid as a cover", and that Soros wanted a [one-world government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One-world_government_conspiracy).[^cite_note-Selk-2018-343][^cite_note-Mirkinson-2010-344] Beck promoted the false and anti-Semitic conspiracy theory that Soros was a [Nazi collaborator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_collaborator) as a 14-year-old in Nazi-occupied Hungary.[^cite_note-345] Beck also characterized Soros's mother as a "wildly anti-Semitic" Nazi collaborator.[^cite_note-Selk-2018-343] According to *The Washington Post*: "Beck's series was largely considered obscene and delusional, if not outright anti-Semitic", but Beck's conspiracy theory became common on the right wing of American politics.[^cite_note-Selk-2018-343] Amid criticism of Beck's false smears, Fox News defended Beck, stating "information regarding Mr. Soros's experiences growing up were taken directly from his writings and from interviews given by him to the media, and no negative opinion was offered as to his actions as a child."[^cite_note-Mirkinson-2010-344][^cite_note-346] Roger Ailes, then-head of Fox News, dismissed criticism levied at Beck by hundreds of rabbis, saying that they were "left-wing rabbis who basically don't think that anybody can ever use the word, Holocaust, on the air."[^cite_note-347]

### COVID-19 pandemic

Main articles: [Fox News coverage of the COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_News_controversies#Coverage_of_the_COVID-19_pandemic) and [Media coverage of the COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_coverage_of_the_COVID-19_pandemic)

During the first few weeks of the [COVID-19 pandemic in the United States](63136490.md), Fox News was considerably more likely than other mainstream news outlets to promote misinformation about COVID-19.[^cite_note-Motta_2020-47] The network promoted the narrative that the emergency response to the pandemic was politically motivated or otherwise unwarranted,[^cite_note-Darcy_2020-48] with [Sean Hannity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sean_Hannity) explicitly calling it a "hoax" (he later denied doing so) and other hosts downplaying it.[^cite_note-Rieger_2020-49] This coverage was consistent with the messaging of Trump at the time.[^cite_note-Wapo2020-03-17-348] Only in mid March did the network change the tone of its coverage,[^cite_note-349] after President Trump declared a national emergency.[^cite_note-Wapo2020-03-17-348] At the same time that Fox News commentators downplayed the threat of the virus in public, Fox's management and the Murdoch family took a broad range of internal measures to protect themselves and their employees against it.[^cite_note-350][^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352][^cite_note-353]

Sean Hannity and Laura Ingraham, two of Fox News's primetime hosts, promoted use of the drug [hydroxychloroquine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydroxychloroquine) for the treatment of COVID-19, an [off-label](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Off-label) usage which at the time was supported only by [anecdotal evidence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anecdotal_evidence), after it was touted by Trump as a possible cure.[^cite_note-354] Fox News promoted a conspiracy theory that coronavirus death toll numbers were inflated with people who would have died anyway from preexisting conditions. This was disputed by White House coronavirus task force members [Anthony Fauci](1227250.md) and [Deborah Birx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deborah_Birx), with Fauci describing conspiracy theories as "nothing but distractions" during public health crises.[^cite_note-355] Later in the pandemic, Hannity, Ingraham and Carlson promoted the use of livestock [dewormer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dewormer) [ivermectin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivermectin) as a possible COVID-19 treatment.[^cite_note-356]

Studies have linked trust in Fox News,[^cite_note-357] as well as viewership of Fox News,[^cite_note-358] with fewer preventive behaviors and more risky behaviors related to COVID-19.[^cite_note-359]

Once a COVID-19 vaccine became widely available, Fox News consistently questioned the efficacy and safety of the vaccine, celebrated evidence-free skepticism, and blasted attempts to promote vaccinations.[^cite_note-360] More than 90% of Fox Corporation's full-time employees had been fully vaccinated by September 2021.[^cite_note-361]

### 2020 election fraud allegations and lawsuits by Dominion and Smartmatic

See also: [Dominion Voting Systems v. Fox News Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominion_Voting_Systems_v._Fox_News_Network) and [Big lie § Donald Trump's false claims of a stolen election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_lie#Donald_Trump's_false_claims_of_a_stolen_election)

After Trump's defeat in the 2020 presidential election, Fox News host [Jeanine Pirro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeanine_Pirro) promoted baseless allegations on her program that voting machine company [Smartmatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smartmatic) and its competitor [Dominion Voting Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominion_Voting_Systems) had conspired to rig the election against Trump. Hosts [Lou Dobbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lou_Dobbs) and [Maria Bartiromo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Bartiromo) also promoted the allegations on their programs on sister network Fox Business.[^cite_note-362] In December 2020, Smartmatic sent a letter to Fox News demanding retractions and threatening legal action, specifying that retractions "must be published on multiple occasions" so as to "match the attention and audience targeted with the original defamatory publications."[^cite_note-363] Days later, each of the three programs aired the same three-minute video segment consisting of an interview with an election technology expert who refuted the allegations promoted by the hosts, responding to questions from an unseen and unidentified man. None of the three hosts personally issued retractions. Smartmatic filed a \$2.7 billion defamation suit against the network, the three hosts, Powell and Trump attorney [Rudy Giuliani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudy_Giuliani) in February 2021.[^cite_note-364] In an April 2021 court brief seeking dismissal of the suit, Fox attorney [Paul Clement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Clement) argued that the network was simply "reporting allegations made by a sitting President and his lawyers."[^cite_note-365] A New York State Supreme Court judge ruled in March 2022 that the suit could proceed, though he dismissed allegations against [Sidney Powell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sidney_Powell) and Pirro, and some claims against Giuliani. The judge allowed allegations against Bartiromo and Dobbs to stand.[^cite_note-366] The [New York Supreme Court, Appellate Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Supreme_Court,_Appellate_Division) unanimously rejected a Fox News bid to dismiss the Smartmatic suit in February 2023. The court reinstated defamation allegations against Giuliani and Pirro.[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-368]

In December 2020, Dominion Voting Systems sent a similar letter demanding retractions to Trump attorney Sidney Powell,[^cite_note-369][^cite_note-370][^cite_note-371] who had promoted the allegations on Fox programs.[^cite_note-372][^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374] On March 26, 2021, Dominion filed a \$1.6 billion defamation lawsuit against Fox News, alleging that Fox and some of its pundits spread conspiracy theories about Dominion, and allowed guests to make false statements about the company.[^cite_note-Izadi-2021-375] On May 18, 2021, Fox News filed a motion to dismiss the Dominion Voting Systems lawsuit, asserting a [First Amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Amendment) right "to inform the public about newsworthy allegations of paramount public concern."[^cite_note-376] The motion to dismiss was denied on December 16, 2021, by a Delaware Superior Court judge.[^cite_note-377] In addition to Bartiromo, Dobbs, and Pirro, the suit also names primetime hosts Tucker Carlson and Sean Hannity.[^cite_note-378] Venezuelan businessman Majed Khalil sued Fox, Dobbs and Powell for \$250 million in December 2021, alleging they had falsely implicated him in rigging Dominion and Smartmatic machines.[^cite_note-379] Dobbs and Fox News reached a confidential settlement with Khalil in April 2023.[^cite_note-380]

Fox News was the only major network or cable news outlet to not carry the first televised [prime time](24973.md) hearing of the [January 6 committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_6_committee) live; its regular programming of *Tucker Carlson Tonight* and *Hannity* was aired without commercial breaks. During the weeks following the election, Carlson and Hannity often amplified Trump's election falsehoods on their programs; previously disclosed text messages between Hannity and White House press secretary [Kayleigh McEnany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayleigh_McEnany) were presented during the hearing. Hannity told his audience, "Unlike this committee and their cheerleaders in the media mob, we will actually be telling you the truth," while Carlson said, "This is the only hour on an American news channel that won't be covering their propaganda live. They are lying and we are not going to help them do it."[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383][^cite_note-384]

In June 2022, a Delaware Superior Court judge again declined to dismiss the Dominion suit against Fox News, and also allowed Dominion to sue the network's corporate parent, Fox Corporation. The judge ruled that Rupert and [Lachlan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lachlan_Murdoch) Murdoch may have acted with [actual malice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Actual_malice) because there was a reasonable inference they "either knew Dominion had not manipulated the election or at least recklessly disregarded the truth when they allegedly caused Fox News to propagate its claims about Dominion." He noted a report that Rupert Murdoch spoke with Trump a few days after the election and informed him that he had lost.[^cite_note-385]

*The New York Times* reported in December 2022 that Dominion had acquired communications between Fox News executives and hosts, and between a Fox Corporation employee and the Trump White House, showing they knew that what the network was reporting was untrue. Dominion attorneys said hosts Sean Hannity and Tucker Carlson, and Fox executives, attested to this in sworn depositions. In November 2020, Hannity hosted [Sidney Powell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sidney_Powell), who asserted Dominion machines had been rigged, but said in his deposition, "I did not believe it for one second."[^cite_note-386] A February 2023 Dominion court filing showed Fox News primetime hosts messaging each other to insult and mock Trump advisers, indicating the hosts knew the allegations made by Powell and Giuliani were false. Rupert Murdoch messaged that Trump's voter fraud claims were "really crazy stuff," telling Fox News CEO Suzanne Scott that it was "terrible stuff damaging everybody, I fear." As a January 2021 Georgia runoff election approached that would determine party control of the U.S. Senate, Murdoch told Scott, "Trump will concede eventually and we should concentrate on Georgia, helping any way we can."[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-388][^cite_note-389][^cite_note-390]

After the 2016 election, the network developed a cutting-edge system to call elections, which proved very successful during the 2018 midterm elections. The network was the first to call the 2020 Arizona race for Biden, angering many viewers. Washington managing editor [Bill Sammon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Sammon) supervised the network's Decision Desk that made the call. [Bret Baier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bret_Baier) and [Martha MacCallum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martha_MacCallum), the network's main news anchors, suggested during a high-level conference call that relying solely on data to make the call was inadequate and that viewer reaction should also be considered; MacCallum said, "in a Trump environment, the game is just very, very different." Sammon stood by the 2020 call and was fired by the network after the January 2021 Georgia runoff.[^cite_note-391]

In 2023, Rupert Murdoch was deposed and testified that some Fox News commentators were endorsing [election fraud claims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_lie#Donald_Trump's_false_claims_of_a_stolen_election) they knew were false.[^cite_note-Matza_2/27/2023-392][^cite_note-Peters_Robertson_2/27/2023-393]

In February 2023, Fox's internal communications were released, showing that its presenters and senior executives privately doubted Donald Trump's claims of a stolen election.[^cite_note-Crazy-394] Chairman Rupert Murdoch once described Trump's voter fraud claims as "really crazy stuff", and also said that Trump advisers Rudy Giuliani and Sidney Powell's television appearances were "terrible stuff damaging everybody".[^cite_note-Crazy-394] One November 2020 exchange showed Tucker Carlson accusing Powell of "lying ... I caught her. It's insane", with Laura Ingraham responding that "Sidney is a complete nut. No one will work with her. Ditto with Rudy".[^cite_note-Crazy-394] In another exchange that month, Carlson called for Fox journalist Jacqui Heinrich to be "fired" because she fact-checked Trump and said that there was no evidence of voter fraud from Dominion.[^cite_note-Crazy-394] Carlson said that Heinrich's actions "needs to stop immediately, like tonight. It's measurably hurting the company. The stock price is down", while Heinrich deleted the fact-check the next morning.[^cite_note-Crazy-394]

In March 2023, more of Fox's internal communications were released.[^cite_note-Levine-395] One November 2020 communication showed Fox CEO Suzanne Scott criticizing fact-checking, stating that she cannot "keep defending these reporters who don't understand our viewers and how to handle stories ... The audience feels like we crapped on" them, and Fox was losing their audience's "trust and belief" in them.[^cite_note-Levine-395] Another December 2020 communication showed Scott responding to Fox presenter Eric Shawn's fact-checking of Donald Trump's false 2020 election claims by demanding that the fact-checking "has to stop now ... This is bad business ... The audience is furious."[^cite_note-Levine-395]

On March 31, 2023, Delaware Superior Court judge Eric Davis ruled in a [summary judgment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summary_judgment) that it "is CRYSTAL clear that none of the statements relating to Dominion about the 2020 election are true" and ordered for the case to go to trial.[^cite_note-crystal-45]

On April 18, 2023, Fox News reached a settlement with Dominion just before the trial started, concluding the lawsuit; Fox agreed to pay Dominion \$787.5 million, and further stated: "We acknowledge the Court's rulings finding certain claims about Dominion to be false".[^cite_note-787m-44]

### Compulsory reductions in meat consumption

In April 2021,[^cite_note-396] at least five Fox News and Fox Business personalities amplified a story published by the *[Daily Mail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daily_Mail)*, a British tabloid, that incorrectly linked a university study to President Joe Biden's climate change agenda, to falsely assert that Americans would be compelled to dramatically reduce their meat consumption to mitigate [greenhouse gas emissions](9528025.md) caused by [flatulence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flatulence). Fox News aired a graphic detailing the supposed compulsory reductions, falsely indicating the information came from the [Agriculture Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Agriculture), which numerous Republican politicians and commentators tweeted. Fox News anchor [John Roberts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Roberts_\(journalist\)) reported to "say goodbye to your burgers if you want to sign up to the Biden climate agenda." Days later, Roberts acknowledged on air that the story was false.[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-398][^cite_note-399]

### Report that Biden administration was continuing to build the Mexico–United States border wall

According to analysis by Media Matters, on May 12, 2021, Fox News reported on its website: "Biden resumes [border wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico%E2%80%93United_States_border_wall) construction after promising to halt it". Correspondent Bill Melugin then appeared on *[Special Report with Bret Baier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Report_with_Bret_Baier)* to report "the [U.S. Army Corps of Engineers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Army_Corps_of_Engineers) is actually going to be restarting border wall construction down in the Rio Grande Valley" after "a lot of blowback and pressure from local residents and local politicians." After the Corps of Engineers tweeted a clarification, Melugin deleted a tweet about the story and tweeted an "update" clarifying that a levee wall was being constructed to mitigate damage to flood control systems caused by uncompleted wall construction, and the website story headline was changed to "Biden administration to resume border wall levee construction as crisis worsens." Later on *Fox News Primetime*, host [Brian Kilmeade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Kilmeade) briefly noted the levee but commented to former Trump advisor [Stephen Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Miller): "They're going to restart building the wall again, Stephen." Fox News host Sean Hannity later broadcast the original Melugin story without any mention of the levee.[^cite_note-400][^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402]

### Crime reporting

[Media Matters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_Matters) reported in September 2024 that during the Biden presidency Fox News had promoted a false "crime crisis" narrative, particularly directed toward undocumented migrants, which reflected Donald Trump's political rhetoric. The Fox News narrative consisted of reported violent crime anecdotes rather than FBI crime rate statistics showing violent crime had declined significantly since 2020. One Fox host, [Ainsley Earhardt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ainsley_Earhardt), said that even if the FBI data were right, "we're all a little bit more scared than we used to be." Later that month, weeks before the 2024 presidential election, the FBI released crime data for 2023 showing that violent crime had declined 3% from 2022. The report was widely covered by mainstream news outlets that day, though the Fox News coverage was limited to a 28-second segment by evening anchor [Bret Baier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bret_Baier). He reported "critics say the report is not accurate because it does not include big cities," echoing a false assertion made by [Elon Musk](909036.md) and other Trump supporters on social media.[^cite_note-403][^cite_note-404]

## Controversies

Main article: [Fox News controversies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_News_controversies)

### Sexual harassment

The network has been accused of permitting [sexual harassment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_harassment) and [racial discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_discrimination) by on-air hosts, executives, and employees,[^cite_note-Guthrie_2017-50][^cite_note-Steel_2017-51][^cite_note-Ember_2017-52] paying out millions of dollars in legal settlements.[^cite_note-Chappell_2016-53][^cite_note-405] Prominent Fox News figures such as Roger Ailes, [Bill O'Reilly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_O'Reilly_\(political_commentator\)) and [Eric Bolling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Bolling) were fired after many women accused them of sexual harassment. At least four lawsuits alleged Fox News co-president [Bill Shine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Shine) ignored, enabled or concealed Roger Ailes' alleged sexual harassment.[^cite_note-Grynbaum-2017c-406][^cite_note-Sakoui-2017-407][^cite_note-408] Fox News CEO Rupert Murdoch has dismissed the high-profile sexual misconduct allegations as "largely political" and speculated they were made "because we are conservative".[^cite_note-409]

Bill O'Reilly and Fox News settled six agreements, totaling \$45 million, with women who accused O'Reilly of sexual harassment.[^cite_note-Steel-2017-410][^cite_note-411] In January 2017, shortly after Bill O'Reilly settled a sexual harassment lawsuit for \$32 million ("an extraordinarily large amount for such cases"), Fox News renewed Bill O'Reilly's contract.[^cite_note-Steel-2017-410] Fox News's parent company, 21st Century Fox, said it was aware of the lawsuit.[^cite_note-Steel-2017-410] The contract between O'Reilly and Fox News read he could not be fired from the network unless sexual harassment allegations were proven in court.[^cite_note-412]

Fox News's extensive coverage of the [Harvey Weinstein scandal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvey_Weinstein_scandal) in October 2017 was seen by some as hypocritical.[^cite_note-Axios-413][^cite_note-Bauder-2017-414] Fox News dedicated at least 12 hours of coverage to the Weinstein scandal, yet only dedicated 20 minutes to Bill O'Reilly, who just like Weinstein had been accused of sexual harassment by a multitude of women.[^cite_note-Axios-413][^cite_note-Bauder-2017-414] A few weeks later, when a number of females under the age of 18, including a 14-year-old, accused Alabama Senate candidate [Roy Moore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roy_Moore) of making sexual advances, Hannity dismissed the sexual misconduct allegations and dedicated coverage on his television show to casting doubt on the accusers.[^cite_note-415][^cite_note-416] Other prime-time Fox News hosts Tucker Carlson and Laura Ingraham queried *[The Washington Post](102226.md)*'s reporting or opted to bring up sexual misconduct allegations regarding show business figures such as Harvey Weinstein and [Louis C.K](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_C.K).[^cite_note-Grynbaum-2017b-205] Fox News figures [Jeanine Pirro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeanine_Pirro) and [Gregg Jarrett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregg_Jarrett) questioned both the validity of *The Washington Post*'s reporting and that of the women.[^cite_note-417] In December 2017, a few days before the Alabama Senate election, Fox News, along with the conspiracy websites *Breitbart News* and *[The Gateway Pundit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Gateway_Pundit)*, ran an inaccurate headline which claimed one of Roy Moore's accusers admitted to forging an inscription by Roy Moore in her yearbook; Fox News later added a correction to the story.[^cite_note-418]

A number of Fox News hosts have welcomed Bill O'Reilly to their shows and paid tributes to Roger Ailes after his death. In May 2017, Hannity called Ailes "a second father" and said to Ailes's "enemies" that he was "preparing to kick your a\*\* in the next life".[^cite_note-Park-2017-419] Ailes had the year before been fired from Fox News after women alleged he sexually harassed them.[^cite_note-Park-2017-419] In September 2017, several months after Bill O'Reilly was fired from Fox News in the wake of women alleging he sexually harassed them, Hannity hosted O'Reilly on his show.[^cite_note-420][^cite_note-Darcy-2017b-421][^cite_note-422] Some Fox News employees criticized the decision.[^cite_note-Darcy-2017b-421] According to CNN, during the interview, Hannity found kinship with O'Reilly as he appeared "to feel that he and O'Reilly have both become victims of liberals looking to silence them."[^cite_note-Darcy-2017b-421]

### Obama administration conflict

In September 2009, the Obama administration engaged in a verbal conflict with Fox News Channel. On September 20, President Barack Obama appeared on all major news programs except Fox News, a snub partially in response to remarks about him by commentators Glenn Beck and Sean Hannity and Fox coverage of Obama's health-care proposal.[^cite_note-423][^cite_note-usnews.com-424]

In late September 2009, Obama's senior advisor [David Axelrod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Axelrod) and Roger Ailes met in secret to attempt to smooth out tensions between the two camps. Two weeks later, White House chief of staff [Rahm Emanuel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rahm_Emanuel) referred to FNC as "not a news network" and communications director [Anita Dunn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anita_Dunn) said "Fox News often operates as either the research arm or the communications arm of the Republican Party".[^cite_note-425][^cite_note-nytimes_2019-10-23-426] Obama commented: "If media is operating basically as a talk radio format, then that's one thing, and if it's operating as a news outlet, then that's another."[^cite_note-427] Emanuel said it was important "to not have the CNNs and the others in the world basically be led in following Fox".[^cite_note-428]

Within days, it was reported that Fox had been excluded from an interview with administration official [Ken Feinberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ken_Feinberg), with bureau chiefs from the White House press pool (ABC, CBS, NBC, and CNN) coming to Fox's defense.[^cite_note-nytimes_2019-10-23-426] A bureau chief said: "If any member had been excluded it would have been the same thing, it has nothing to do with Fox or the White House or the substance of the issues."[^cite_note-429] Shortly after the story broke, the White House admitted to a low-level mistake, saying Fox had not made a specific request to interview Feinberg. Fox White House correspondent Major Garrett said he had not made a specific request, but had a "standing request from me as senior White House correspondent on Fox to interview any newsmaker at the Treasury at any given time news is being made".[^cite_note-430]

On November 8, 2009, the *[Los Angeles Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Times)* reported an unnamed Democratic consultant was warned by the White House not to appear on Fox News again. According to the article, Dunn claimed in an e-mail to have checked with colleagues who "deal with TV issues" who denied telling anyone to avoid Fox. [Patrick Caddell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Caddell), a Fox News contributor and former pollster for President [Jimmy Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Carter), said he had spoken with other Democratic consultants who had received similar warnings from the White House.[^cite_note-431]

On October 2, 2013, Fox News host [Anna Kooiman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna_Kooiman) cited on the air a fake story from the *National Report* parody site, which claimed Obama had offered to keep the International Museum of Muslim Cultures open with cash from his own pocket.[^cite_note-432][^cite_note-433]

### Journalistic ethical standards

Fox News attracted controversy in April 2018 when it was revealed primetime host Sean Hannity had defended Trump's then personal attorney Michael Cohen on air without disclosing Cohen was his lawyer.[^cite_note-No_Disclosure-NYT-434] On April 9, 2018, federal agents from the [U.S. Attorney's office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Attorney's_Office_for_the_Southern_District_of_New_York) served a search warrant on Cohen's office and residence.[^cite_note-435] On the air, Hannity defended Cohen and criticized the federal action, calling it "highly questionable" and "an unprecedented abuse of power".[^cite_note-Winter-436] On April 16, 2018, in a court hearing, Cohen's lawyers told the judge that Cohen had ten clients in 2017–2018 but did "traditional legal tasks" for only three, including Trump.[^cite_note-Miller-437][^cite_note-438][^cite_note-439] The federal judge ordered the revelation of the third client, whom Cohen's lawyers named as Hannity.[^cite_note-Miller-437]

Hannity was not sanctioned by Fox News for this breach of journalistic ethics, with Fox News releasing a statement that the channel was unaware of Hannity's relationship to Cohen and that it had "spoken to Sean and he continues to have our full support."[^cite_note-No_Disclosure-NYT-434][^cite_note-440][^cite_note-441][^cite_note-cohenCNBC-442] Media ethics experts said that Hannity's disclosure failure was a major breach of journalistic ethics and that the network should have suspended or fired him for it.[^cite_note-cohenCNBC-442]

### NYC Human Rights Law violations

In mid-2021, Fox News agreed to pay a \$1 million settlement to New York City after its [Commission on Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commission_on_Human_Rights) cited "a pattern of violating the [NYC Human Rights Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Human_Rights_Law)". A Fox News spokesperson claimed that "FOX News Media has already been in full compliance across the board, but \[settled] to continue enacting extensive preventive measures against all forms of discrimination and harassment."[^cite_note-443]

## International transmission

World map, with countries carrying terrestrial FNC in red and satellite providers in orange

Countries where Fox News is provided

The Fox News Channel feed has international availability via multiple providers, while *Fox Extra* segments provide alternate programming.[^cite_note-444] Fox News is carried in more than 40 countries.

### Australia

In Australia, FNC is broadcast on the dominant pay television provider [Foxtel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foxtel).

### Brazil

FNC reached Brazil through [Sky Brasil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_Brasil) on November 1, 2002, after being introduced at ABTA 2002.[^cite_note-445] Commercials on FNC are replaced with *Fox Extra*. It is available on [Vivo TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vivo_TV).

### Canada

Fox had initially planned to launch a joint venture with [Canwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canwest)'s [Global Television Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Television_Network), tentatively named Fox News Canada, which would have featured a mixture of U.S. and [Canadian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_content) news programming.[^cite_note-446] As a result, the [CRTC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CRTC) denied a 2003 application requesting permission for Fox News Channel to be [carried in Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_foreign_television_channels_available_in_Canada). However, in March 2004, a Fox executive said the venture had been shelved; in November of that year, the CRTC added Fox News to its whitelist of foreign channels that may be carried by television providers.[^cite_note-447]

In May 2023, the CRTC announced that it would open a public consultation regarding the channel's carriage in Canada, acting upon complaints by the LGBT advocacy group [Egale Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egale_Canada) surrounding an episode of *Tucker Carlson Tonight* that contained content described as "malicious misinformation" regarding trans, non-binary, gender non-conforming, and [two-spirit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-spirit) communities, including "the inflammatory and false claim that trans people are 'targeting' Christians."[^cite_note-448]

### India

It is available through streaming service [Disney+ Hotstar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney+_Hotstar).

### Indonesia

In [Indonesia](14579.md), it is available on Channel 397 on pay-TV provider [First Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Media_\(Indonesian_media_company\)).

### Ireland

*See: [United Kingdom & Ireland](#United_Kingdom_and_Ireland).*

### Israel

In Israel, FNC is broadcast on Channel 105 of the satellite provider [Yes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yes_\(Israel\)), as well as being carried on Cellcom TV and Partner TV.[^cite_note-449] It is also broadcast on channel 200 on cable operator [HOT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HOT_\(Israel\)).[^cite_note-450]

### Italy

In Italy, FNC is broadcast on [Sky Italia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_Italia). Fox News was launched on [Stream TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stream_TV) in 2001, and moved to Sky Italia in 2003.

### Japan

Although service to Japan ceased in summer 2003, it can still be seen on Americable (distributor for [American bases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Forces_Japan)),[^cite_note-histel-451] Mediatti ([Kadena Air Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadena_Air_Base))[^cite_note-452] and Pan Global TV Japan.[^cite_note-453]

### Mexico

The channel's international feed is being carried by cable provider [Izzi Telecom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Izzi_Telecom).

### Netherlands

In the Netherlands, Fox News has been carried by cable providers [UPC Nederland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UPC_Nederland) and CASEMA, and satellite provider [Canaldigitaal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canaldigitaal); all have dropped the channel in recent years. At this time, only cable provider Caiway (available in a limited number of towns in the central part of the country) is broadcasting the channel. The channel was also carried by IPTV provider KNIPPR (owned by [T-Mobile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T-Mobile_International_AG)).

### New Zealand

In New Zealand, FNC is broadcast on Channel 088 of pay satellite operator [SKY Network Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SKY_Network_Television)'s digital platform. It was formerly broadcast overnight on free-to-air [UHF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UHF) New Zealand TV channel [Prime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Television_\(New_Zealand\)); this was discontinued in January 2010, reportedly due to an expiring broadcasting license.[^cite_note-454]

### Pakistan

In Pakistan, Fox News Channel is available on [PTCL Smart TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PTCL_Smart_TV) and a number of cable and [IPTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPTV) operators.

### Philippines

In the [Philippines](23440.md), Fox News Channel is available on [Sky Cable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_Cable), [Cablelink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cablelink) and [G Sat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G_Sat) Channel 50. It was available on [Cignal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cignal) until January 1, 2021, due to contract expiration; however, the channel returned on June 16, 2022.

### Portugal

In Portugal, Fox News was available on [Meo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MEO_\(telecommunication_company\)). The channel is however no longer available on the operator and it is not carried by other Portuguese TV operators.

### Scandinavia

Between 2003 and 2006, in Sweden and the other [Scandinavian countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scandinavian_countries), FNC was broadcast 16 hours a day on [TV8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV8_\(Sweden\)) (with *Fox News Extra* segments replacing U.S. advertising). Fox News was dropped by TV8 in September 2006.

### Singapore

In Singapore, FNC is broadcast on pay-TV operator [StarHub TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/StarHub_TV), as well on [Singtel TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singtel_TV).

### South Africa

In South Africa, FNC is broadcast on [StarSat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/StarSat).[^cite_note-455] The most popular pay television operator, [DStv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DStv), does not offer FNC in its channel bouquet.[^cite_note-456]

### Spain

In Spain, Fox News was available on [Movistar Plus+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Movistar_Plus+). The channel was part of the operator since its first incarnation as [Canal Satellite Digital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canal+_\(Spanish_TV_provider\)) in the early 2000s, but was later removed from the operator's satellite offer by March 2023, and ceased transmission to the remaining offers on July 9, 2024.[^cite_note-457] The channel is not carried by other Spanish TV operators.

### United Kingdom and Ireland

FNC was carried in the [United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_in_the_United_Kingdom) by [Sky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_\(UK_and_Ireland\)). On August 29, 2017, Sky dropped Fox News; the broadcaster said its carriage was not "commercially viable" due to average viewership of fewer than 2,000 viewers per day. The company said the decision was unrelated to 21st Century Fox's proposed acquisition of the remainder of [Sky plc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_plc) (which ultimately led to a bidding war that resulted in its acquisition by [Comcast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comcast) instead).[^cite_note-458]

The potential co-ownership had prompted concerns from critics of the deal, who felt [Sky News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_News) could similarly undergo a shift to an opinionated format with a right-wing viewpoint. However, such a move would violate [Ofcom](411296.md) broadcast codes, which requires all news programming to show due impartiality. The channel's broadcasts in the country have violated this rule on several occasions.[^cite_note-459][^cite_note-460][^cite_note-461][^cite_note-462]

[^cite_note-Jamieson-2010-23]: &#x20;Jamieson, Kathleen Hall; Cappella, Joseph N. (February 4, 2010). [*Echo Chamber: Rush Limbaugh and the Conservative Media Establishment*](https://global.oup.com/academic/product/echo-chamber-9780195398601). Oxford, New York: Oxford University Press. [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [978-0-19539-860-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/978-0-19539-860-1). [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20200612104608/https://global.oup.com/academic/product/echo-chamber-9780195398601?cc=us\&lang=en&) from the original on June 12, 2020. Retrieved May 11, 2018. We do this to illustrate the ways Fox News, Limbaugh, and the print and web editorial pages of the Wall Street Journal play both offense and defense in service of conservative objectives. As these case studies will suggest, the big three reinforce each other's conservative messages in ways that distinguish them from the other major broadcast media, CBS News, NBC News, ABC News, CNN, MSNBC, CNBC and major print outlets such as the Washington Post and New York Times.
